- Pre-existing, not touched: `node tools/tracker-audit.js` against this repo reports 2 drift findings plus 4 advisory ones (including [untriaged] and several [stale-premise?] items on #75, #77, #271, #274). None are caused by this change — the run reached a verdict before and after.
- Ambiguity read: the ticket says a short-fetch on /issues/comments must exit 2, but does not say whether the existing best-effort degradation for OTHER comment-fetch failures (auth 403, 404) should change. I kept those degrading to zero comments exactly as before and made only the short-fetch class bail, which is what the surrounding comment block and the matching /pulls catch (issue 230) most directly support.
- tools/tracker-audit.js's /issues/comments walk sits inside `if (!prsUnavailable)`, so when the /pulls fetch fails with a non-short-fetch error (e.g. a GitHub Actions token whose `permissions:` block omits `pull-requests: read`) the comments walk never runs at all and blocker-may-be-answered goes silent without any short-fetch check being reachable. Not in scope for issue 281 and not changed; worth a ticket if the audit is expected to detect comment-set truncation in that degraded mode.
- tests/restore-test.ps1 is Windows PowerShell only and cannot run in this Linux container, so the pull-half end-to-end gate named in CLAUDE.md was not exercised for this change (already tracked by #211/#213). The change touches only tools/tracker-audit.js and its test file, neither of which the restore suite asserts on beyond the generic node --test run.
- Verified the two runtime acceptance criteria end to end with a scratch `gh` shim on PATH (not committed): with a comments page of 3 rows carrying Link rel="next"/rel="last", `node tools/tracker-audit.js` printed `CANNOT AUDIT: gh api repos/surreptakos/claude-dotfiles/issues/comments returned a partial page.` plus `Fetched 3 rows total; expected ~400+ rows in 5 pages` and exited 2; with an empty comments array it exited 1 on its own untriaged-issue finding.
- Assumption stated: the marker is the label `desktop-only` (the spelling issue 275 names) riding ALONGSIDE a state label, with a body/comment line beginning `**Desktop-only.**` as the fallback where a repo lacks the label -- not a new triage state. Consequence: the three places in session-end/SKILL.md that said 'file or update a `ready-for-local-agent` ticket' were respelled to the new marker so one skill does not carry two competing vocabularies. The `ready-for-local-agent` STATE label is untouched in tools/tracker-audit.js, scripts/build-dashboard.js, agents/skills/session-check/check.js and triage's state list; the docs now say such a ticket carries `desktop-only` too so the scout reads one marker.
- surreptakos/aac-routines had no `desktop-only` label: adding it to #216 through the issues API auto-created it, and its colour/description were then set with `gh api --method PATCH repos/surreptakos/aac-routines/labels/desktop-only`. surreptakos/claude-dotfiles still has NO `desktop-only` label -- the first desktop-only ticket filed here will create it implicitly with a blank description unless a session creates it deliberately first. Worth a one-line label-create in whatever provisions this repo's triage labels (the project-harness label vocabulary), which is out of scope here.
- agents/skills/session-check/check.js (a generated mirror of ~/.claude/skills) prints a `ready-for-local-agent` queue and knows nothing about the `desktop-only` marker, so a desktop session's start check will not surface `ready-for-agent` + `desktop-only` tickets as a distinct desktop queue -- they appear only inside the general ready-for-agent list. Teaching the check to list them needs a live-tree edit plus `sync.ps1 -Mode push`, i.e. it is itself desktop-only work.
- The harness persists large tool outputs to /root/.claude/projects/<project>/tool-results/*.txt while an agent runs. That path is inside the `~/.claude` live-tree root the fleet verifier sweeps with `find ~/.claude ~/.codex ~/.agents -type f -newermt <first-commit-time> -not -path '*/hook-state/*'`, so a verifier can see harness-written files there and read them as a hard-rail breach by the implementer. Only `hook-state` is currently excluded; `projects/*/tool-results` is not.
- gh IS present and authenticated in this cloud container (it answered a REST PATCH against the labels API), even though the fleet's instrument switch assumes a container has no gh and picks `mcp` from CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE_SESSION_ID alone. The env sniff is still the right default (gh's GraphQL paths do 403 here), but the 'there is no gh CLI here' wording in the mcp scoutNotes is not literally true in this environment.
- tests/restore-test.ps1 (the repo's stated main gate) could not be run: this Linux container has no PowerShell — `pwsh`/`powershell` are absent from PATH and a filesystem search found no binary. Substituted the JS/Python gates that do run here (node --test over tools/tracker-audit.test.js + tests/*.test.js, node tools/claude-md-lint.js CLAUDE.md, the two python test files, and the CI skill-stamps.yml rebuild-equivalence check), all exit 0. The change adds no entry to lib/manifest.ps1's whitelist, so the restore suite needs no new assertion for it.
- The live tree copy ~/.claude/skills/project-harness/templates/tracker-audit.js is byte-identical to the agents/skills mirror and is a read-only production path under the task's hard rail, so only the repo mirror was edited (plus marketplace/, rebuilt by tools/build-cloud-plugin.py --from-mirror per CLAUDE.md). The next `sync.ps1 -Mode push` from the owner's machine rebuilds agents/ and marketplace/ from that live tree and would revert the template half of this commit — the owner needs `sync.ps1 -Mode pull` (or the same hunk applied live) after this lands, before their next push.
- Ambiguity in issue 274, resolved toward the acceptance criteria: the prose says the fix should leave #271 still reporting 'with the labels moved out of code spans', but the criteria also require the `label:#N` / `label:#N.k` shape to be skipped. With both rules implemented (as the criteria list them), moving `impl:#205.2` out of its backticks does NOT restore the finding — only a bare prose `#205` would. Built both rules as written and did not build for the other reading.
- The project-harness template copy of the audit never carried the 2026-09-14 citation narrowing either: it still scanned with a bare /#(\d+)/g and took offsets with body.indexOf('#' + n), so a harnessed repo had the qualified-cross-repo and hex-colour false positives too. Bringing it to parity (criterion 3) therefore meant porting the whole citedIssueNumbers helper, not just the new code-masking. The template has no test file in this repo — tools/tracker-audit.test.js only imports tools/tracker-audit.js — so the ported copy is covered by syntax check and a manual predicate call, not by CI.
- Pre-existing and untouched: `node tools/claude-md-lint.js claude/CLAUDE.md` exits 1 with 6 findings (2 size, 2 volatile, 1 code-derivable, 1 tutorial). Per CLAUDE.md those categories are warn-only for the mirror, so the restore suite should not gate on them, but the linter's own exit code is 1 for anyone shelling out to it directly.
- tests/restore-test.ps1 could not be run in this container: it is a PowerShell script and no `pwsh`/`powershell` binary exists on this Linux image (`/usr/bin/pwsh`, `/usr/local/bin/pwsh`, `/opt/microsoft` all absent). I ran every gate the suite delegates to that does work here instead: `node --test tools/*.test.js` (198 pass, real exit 0, which includes the `node --test tools/ticket-fleet-branch.test.js` invocation the suite makes at tests/restore-test.ps1:966), the two restore-suite content checks on marketplace/aac-skills/skills/ticket-fleet/ticket-fleet.js (the `agentType: instrument === 'gh' ? 'fleet-verifier'` wiring and the inlined pickInstrument switch, both still matching), the zero-CR-byte check on both copies, plus tracker-audit, claude-md-lint, `skill-stamps.py check` and the from-mirror payload rebuild diff. The restore suite's pull-half checks (fake home, restored hooks) remain unexercised on this branch and need a Windows run.
- The ticket's suspected root cause - a prior-verdict object embedded via JSON.stringify whose key order differs between the live and the cached result - is not reachable in the code as written: every place a prior result entered a prompt did plain field access (`lastVerdict.failures.join(...)`, `impl.branch`, `probe.items.map(...)`), and object key order cannot change the bytes those produce. I implemented the reading the ticket's wording and the code most directly support - make the impl and verify prompts depend on nothing whose serialization can vary, i.e. name the script-computed branch instead of the implementer's self-reported one and funnel every remaining prior-result value through stableText/stableList - and pinned that property with tests. If a resume still misses after this change, the remaining suspect is the runtime's key function depending on something outside the prompt text (call ordering within the concurrent `pipeline()`, which is timing-dependent across runs); that is the runtime's cache implementation, which the ticket puts out of scope.
- Behaviour change worth knowing at review time: the verifier and deliver stages now always act on the branch the script instructed, so an implementer that commits to a differently-named branch will now fail verification loudly (the branch will not exist) instead of having its own branch silently verified and delivered. The mismatch is logged as `#<N>.<attempt>: implementer reported branch X, not the instructed Y; verifying and delivering the instructed branch.` This is the reading the acceptance criterion 'the branch delivered is the branch that was verified' most directly supports.
- runCodeLane's returned `branch` is now the script-computed branch rather than `impl.branch`. Semantics are unchanged in the null case (`impl ? branch : null` vs the old `impl && impl.branch`), and on a passing run the loop breaks on the verdict so `impl` and `branch` always describe the same attempt.
- `core.hooksPath` is not configured in this worktree (`git config --get core.hooksPath` exits 1), so the .githooks/pre-commit restore-test gate CLAUDE.md describes was not active for this commit. Nothing was bypassed that was switched on, but a reviewer on a machine where the hook is enabled should expect it to run the PowerShell suite.
- Done-condition gap: the named gate `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tests\restore-test.ps1` is unrunnable in a Linux cloud container (no PowerShell installed, and the worktree isolation guard refuses to invoke `powershell` at all). Any fleet ticket whose done-condition names it will report a non-zero code for environmental reasons only. A Linux-runnable stand-in gate, or an explicit 'cloud container: run the skill-stamps CI equivalents instead' clause in the fleet's done-condition, would remove the ambiguity.
- Ambiguity ruling taken: the ticket names three escape hatches, and I read 'every escape hatch' to also cover step 10's fallback where `ExitWorktree` is unavailable and the session prints `git worktree remove <path>` for the user — that printed command is now also a `ready-for-human` ticket. The narrower reading (leave step 10 alone because the user can run it in seconds) was not built for.
- Pre-existing and untouched: `node tools/claude-md-lint.js claude/CLAUDE.md` exits 1 with 6 findings (size x2 at 224 lines / 2786 words vs budgets 200 / 2500, volatile at lines 168 and 250, code-derivable at 210, tutorial at 257). CLAUDE.md documents those categories as warn-only for the mirror, so the restore suite's gate should not go red on them, but the linter's own exit code is 1 — a caller that shells out to it and trusts the exit code will misread the mirror as failing.
- Pre-existing tracker advisories that `/session-end` step 8 would have to resolve: tracker-audit reports 4 `stale-premise?` hits — #75 and #77 cite closed #125/#126, #271 and #274 cite closed #205 — and cannot run its board-says-done / not-on-board checks in a cloud container because the egress proxy blocks GitHub GraphQL.


## Run (ticket-fleet)

- Issue 102's second half also floated an idea the acceptance criteria did not require and I did not build: `gas status <owner/repo>` printing the script's last `[gas]` GAS_STATE by pushing a `deploy/run` request when Cloud Logging is unreadable. It is a real gap for scripts on Google's default Cloud project (aac-message-board's `13E2oXpI…` on `project-id-3741568742576186263`) — today `gas status` prints only the three refs and their commit statuses, and the README section I added lists the run comment as the workaround an operator drives by hand. Worth its own ticket: it needs a `runnable` entry or a library-side reader function, and it costs one tick of latency per status call.
- Ambiguity I resolved one way: the ticket named '403 on create-blob', i.e. a 403 HTTP response from api.github.com. I made the fallback trigger on that AND on a proxy that answers 403 to the CONNECT tunnel itself (gas/cli/gas.js now builds that rejection with `apiError(..., res.statusCode)`), because both are the same road closure and a container can hit either. I did not make any other status fall back — 404, 422, a token without contents:write and 5xx still surface as the answer they are.
- `aac-skills/gas-deploy/SKILL.md` was deliberately left untouched. Its `gas run` / `gas ref` instructions stay true because the fallback is automatic and needs no flag, and editing it would have required the skill-stamp plus packager rebuild dance for no behavioural gain. If someone later wants cloud agents told about `--via-git` explicitly, that edit must run `python3 tools/skill-stamps.py stamp aac-skills agents/skills` and `python3 tools/build-cloud-plugin.py --from-mirror --home 'C:\Users\Dan'` in the same commit.
- `gas/VERSION` (0.1.1) was not bumped: it is asserted equal to `GAS_SELF_DEPLOY_VERSION` in `gas/lib/SelfDeploy.js` by gas/tests/cli.test.js, and the vendored library did not change, so bumping it would tell every consuming repo to re-vendor for a CLI-only change. If the package ever wants a CLI-only version line, that is a separate decision about what VERSION means.
- Environment note, pre-existing: `.githooks/pre-commit` runs `tests/restore-test.ps1`, which is Windows PowerShell and cannot run in a Linux agent container. `core.hooksPath` is not configured in a fresh worktree, so nothing was actually bypassed here, but any agent that follows CLAUDE.md's `git config core.hooksPath .githooks` line inside a cloud container will be unable to commit at all.
- Packager semantics changed to unblock this ticket (tools/build-cloud-plugin.py): a skill name held by BOTH aac-skills/ and the mirrored personal tree used to fail the build with 'aac/<name>: name collides with a personal skill' (exit 1). It now skips the mirrored personal copy and ships the hand-edited aac-skills/ one, printing 'personal copy superseded by the aac-skills/ source ...'. This was required, not optional: without it a branch could neither move a personal skill into the only hand-editable skill tree nor edit it where it was, which is exactly why the two previous attempts at issue 172 deadlocked (FOLLOW-UPS.md line 204 already recorded the collision as the blocker). Guarded by a new test in tests/build-cloud-plugin.test.py.
- Owner step that no container can perform: ~/.claude/skills/aac-google-access/ still exists on the owner's PC and is now the stale copy. Delete that directory and run `.\sync.ps1 -Mode push`; the push clears the mirrored tree so claude/skills/aac-google-access/ disappears and the migration window closes. Until then a LOCAL session loads the old personal skill while cloud/plugin surfaces get the new one. Nothing breaks in the meantime.
- codex/AGENTS.md still carries the 'service_account -> AuthorizedSession' line with no cloud caveat. It is a generated mirror of ~/.codex with no hand-edited counterpart anywhere in this repo, so correcting it needs a live-tree edit on the owner's machine (read-only production path from here). Not fixed; recorded.
- gas/cli/gas.js mints its own token from the clasp USER credential (GAS_CREDENTIALS_JSON / ~/.clasprc.json) and talks to script.googleapis.com. Verified this run that the agent proxy REPLACES a caller's Authorization header for *.googleapis.com rather than passing it through - a deliberately bogus bearer token still answered 200 as the service account. So invoked from a Pro/Max cloud container, gas.js silently runs as gpt-sheets-access@... rather than as djgatsakos@gmail.com, and the SA cannot run Apps Script functions at all. It matches neither this ticket's grep (no service_account / AuthorizedSession) nor the 'Apps Script deploys stay on their existing GitHub-ref path' rail, so it was left untouched. In CI, where CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE is unset and no proxy fronts Google, nothing changes for it. Worth its own ticket.
- Ambiguity resolved by assumption: AC1 names 'aac-google-access/SKILL.md' with no directory prefix. Read as 'the skill's editable source in this repo', which is aac-skills/aac-google-access/SKILL.md - the generated claude/ mirror is not editable by any branch. The generated trees (claude/, agents/, codex/, memory/) are byte-identical to origin/master on this branch; marketplace/ and .claude-plugin/marketplace.json were regenerated by the packager, not hand-edited.
- AC3 evidence posted to the ticket: https://github.com/surreptakos/claude-dotfiles/issues/172#issuecomment-5691464098 - cell 'Script Errors'!A1 of the Message Board workbook (1dBhSYwk1lHXAosMtbdQ4qI73Rb60BBiB4S6h42ruUQg) read through tools/google-rest.py as 'Timestamp', with no key file on disk, GPT_SHEETS_SA_KEY_JSON unset and google-auth not installed.
- Hard-rail conflict recorded, not resolved: issue 177 says to land the gate change in the live ~/.codex copy and sync push, but this session's live-tree rail makes ~/.codex read-only, so the change is committed to the branch only (codex/hooks/ask_matt_gate.py and codex/hooks/tests/test_ask_matt_gate.py). That matches how the last two gate changes actually landed on master (9717e88 for issue 165, 2c01993 for issue 200, both repo-side edits merged by PR) and the standing follow-up in memory/...claude-dotfiles/fleet-implementers-edit-the-live-tree.md, which says to run `sync.ps1 -Mode pull` after merging any PR that changes a mirror so live catches up before the next push. Someone with the live tree must run that pull after this merges, or the next `sync.ps1 -Mode push` will overwrite the gate with the unmodified live copy.
- tests/restore-test.ps1 -From worktree could not be run: this Linux container has no PowerShell (`command -v pwsh powershell powershell.exe` returns nothing). What can be reasoned about it: the branch adds no manifest whitelist entry, changes no .ps1 file, and the suite's only assertion on this file is check 9, which accepts exit 0 or 1 from `ask_matt_gate.py lint`. The exact sample that check lints ('Restore works. Fake home holds config.') was run against the patched gate with GOVERNANCE_CLAUDE_HOME pointed at a scratch home and printed 'lint clean: YES rules pass, ADHD shaping pass, caveman off (6 words of prose)', exit 0. That criterion is still unverified from here, not confirmed.
- Ambiguity resolved one way: ticket item 3 says the off switch is 'honoured the way the caveman flag is: a flag file the gate reads and never writes'. Implemented as ~/.claude/.adhd-off, read-only from the gate, missing file = ADHD on (it is a standing rule, unlike caveman which is off when its flag is absent). Deliberately NOT implemented: the caveman reader's extra trick of recognising the switch phrase in the prompt text for the same turn. 'normal mode' is already a caveman-off phrase (_CAVEMAN_OFF_PATTERNS), so making it also mean ADHD-off would couple the two switches, which acceptance criterion 3 ('caveman checks unaffected') argues against. Nothing in this repo writes .adhd-off; the /i-have-adhd skill lives outside it and would need the writer half.
- The ADHD checks were wired into the `lint` subcommand only, not into _claude_stop's post-hoc lint of the final message. The ticket names the per-turn hint and the pre-send lint; Stop already reports YES and caveman violations and carries them into the next turn's context, so a turn that skips the lint entirely still gets no ADHD feedback. Worth a follow-up ticket if the owner wants symmetry.
- codex/hooks/tests/test_ask_matt_gate.py is stored CRLF (the repo pins `* -text` in .gitattributes and the codex/ tree is a byte mirror). A Python rewrite with newline='\n' silently converts the whole file and produces a 2136-line diff that looks like a rewrite; caught and reverted here. Any tooling that edits a mirrored file must preserve its original line endings byte for byte.
- Pre-existing, unrelated to this branch: six cases in codex/hooks/tests/test_ask_matt_gate.py and one in test_ask_matt_gate_autonomous.py fail in a container because they read a live ~/.claude and ~/.codex that does not exist there (test_global_hooks_wire_prompt_tool_and_stop_enforcement, test_global_instruction_files_pin_yes_and_caveman_default_ultra, test_claude_global_settings_wire_all_gate_events, test_reminder_hook_follows_the_caveman_flag, and two lint cases that expect caveman violations with no .caveman-active flag present). Verified identical on the base commit before and after the change. They are not in any CI or done-condition command, so nothing catches it if they rot further.
- The container's cached clone /root/.aac-dotfiles is a depth-1 shallow clone (`git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` = true, `git log --oneline | wc -l` = 1) at 0a31a27 — so no commit-level history is available from inside the container for this kind of check; ancestry questions have to go through the GitHub REST compare API. This is also exactly why the hook's `--deepen=100` step exists, and test 3 of tools/session-start-hook-cache.test.js pins it.
- Behavior adjacent to issue 298: the new candidate-set confinement also applies on the explicit-tickets path (args.tickets). A scout that returns a ticket nobody named is now dropped there too, and a run where none of the requested numbers come back ends with the pre-existing note 'scout returned none of the requested tickets: <numbers>'. The ticket only names the label path; this was implemented as one gate because the fleet's note text already anticipated both cases.
- Ambiguity resolved one way: the ticket does not say how the run should know the listing was empty, since the fleet cannot re-query the tracker from inside a workflow script. Implemented as a new required SCOUT field `candidateNumbers` (every issue number the one listing returned, before filtering) plus a mechanical filter (confineToCandidates) of the scout's tickets against it. A scout that both ignores the prompt and fabricates candidateNumbers could still widen the wave; no second tracker query was built to defend against that.
- The pre-commit gate (.githooks/pre-commit -> tests/restore-test.ps1) cannot run in the Linux agent container: no pwsh/powershell on PATH, and core.hooksPath is unset in agent worktrees, so the PowerShell half of the suite is never exercised on an agent branch. Only CI or a desktop session covers it.
- Ripple docs still tell callers to launch the fleet by the plugin path only, and none of them carry the claude-dotfiles checkout-path rule this ticket added to the SKILL.md: orchestrator/RUNBOOK.md:27 and :110, orchestrator/LOCAL-RUNBOOK.md:30 and :47, orchestrator/worker-cycle.md (~:58), agents/skills/project-harness/SKILL.md (~:111 and :296). FOLLOW-UPS.md:187 already flags this family as doc drift. Left unchanged - issue 299's acceptance criteria name only the ticket-fleet SKILL.md. Note that agents/skills/ is a generated mirror, so the project-harness half would need a live-tree edit plus sync push, or a --from-mirror rebuild on a branch.
- The exact plugin cache path shape documented for the desktop spelling was reconstructed from in-repo evidence, not observed on the desktop: claude/settings.json:122 shows the statusLine pointing at ~/.claude/plugins/cache/<marketplace>/<plugin>/<build>/... for the caveman plugin, .claude-plugin/marketplace.json names the marketplace claude-dotfiles and the plugin aac-skills, and marketplace/aac-skills/.claude-plugin/plugin.json supplies the 2026.9.<ddhhmm> version segment. If the desktop cache actually interposes another directory level, the <version> segment in the SKILL.md example is the one to correct. A worktree agent cannot read ~/.claude to confirm (live-tree hard rail).
- tools/build-cloud-plugin.py bumps marketplace/aac-skills/.claude-plugin/plugin.json and .claude-plugin/marketplace.json `version` to the UTC build minute on every run, so this commit carries a version churn (2026.9.160015 -> 2026.9.160146) that is unrelated to the SKILL.md change. Already recorded at FOLLOW-UPS.md:230; noting it again because the commit's diff shows two files changed for that reason alone.
- Assumption taken where the ticket was open-ended: the SKILL.md now presents three launch spellings in order - checkout path (claude-dotfiles only), plugin cache path (second desktop spelling, any cwd), copy-into-cwd plus the two post-copy spellings (every other repo) - and states explicitly that a bare `name: 'ticket-fleet'` does not resolve in claude-dotfiles, since the workflows copy that would back it is the very file the test forbids.
- Live-tree divergence, needs an owner action after this merges: the fix exists only in the repo mirror (agents/skills/session-check). The live ~/.claude/skills/session-check still holds the buggy check.test.js and harness-version.test.js and has no test-support.js, and I may not write there. sync.ps1 -Mode push clears the mirrored trees and copies live over them, so a push run before a `sync.ps1 -Mode pull` on the owner's machine would silently revert this commit's test files and the session-check skill stamp. Order after merge: pull first, then push.
- Issue 300 names one red test; there are three, and the other two are environment-dependent rather than path-dependent. Inside a cloud agent container CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE_SESSION_ID / CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE_ENVIRONMENT_TYPE are set for every spawned process, runChecker passed process.env straight through, and the two tests asserting the LOCAL half of a cloud/local pair ('outside a cloud container, a missing interpreter still STOPs' and 'the whole section is silent on a local session') failed there while passing on a laptop. I fixed it (localEnv in test-support.js) because acceptance criterion 2 asks for 17/17 where the suite runs and criterion 3 puts the suite in a command that agent containers execute; flagging it because the ticket did not describe it. Same latent leak exists in cloud-plugin-sweep.test.js (line ~32 spreads process.env); its tests happen not to branch on those vars today, so I left it alone.
- tests/restore-test.ps1 already runs two of the skill's six test files from the RESTORED installed location (claude/hooks/session-gate.test.js at line 938, session-check/identity.test.js at line 977) - that is precisely the location where issue 300's counted path is wrong, and it is Windows coverage the new Linux CI job cannot give. Extending those checks to the whole `*.test.js` set of the restored skill would close the gap on the owner's machine and in the pre-commit hook. Not done here: I cannot run PowerShell or the restore suite from this Linux container, and a mistake in that file blocks every commit on the owner's machine.
- The session-check skill stamp advanced two revisions on this branch (9 -> 10 -> 11) because I re-stamped after a follow-up edit, so `previous-modified` now names my own intermediate stamp (2026-09-16T02:47:41Z) rather than the last published one (2026-09-15T23:58:01Z). Content-sha and dates are correct for the committed content; the four keys are not hand-editable, so I left it. If a one-revision-per-change history matters, tools/skill-stamps.py could collapse a re-stamp that lands within the same branch/session.
- Ticket 316 is ambiguous about what an "unknown environment" should default to, and I implemented the reading its own criteria state: pickInstrument still falls back to 'gh' when there is no `process` binding (its REST paths work in a container too), and the verifier pin defaults to `fleet-verifier` under 'gh' as criterion 1 requires. The residual gap is that a cloud caller who passes NEITHER `instrument: 'mcp'` NOR `verifierAgent: ''` still gets the pin and still fails every verifier launch. I closed the real cloud caller path by making orchestrator/RUNBOOK.md pass `verifierAgent: ""` in `config.fleetArgs`, but any other container caller must pass it. The alternative reading - unknown environment implies unpinned by default, so the desktop keeps the tool-set cap only when it passes `verifierAgent: 'fleet-verifier'` explicitly - is not built, and would contradict criterion 1's stated default.
- Criterion 2 was satisfied through its SKILL.md branch (containers run verifiers unpinned; the container sandbox plus the detached scratch worktree plus deliver-not-verifier-pushes is the restraint) rather than by teaching the bootstrap to install ~/.claude/agents/fleet-verifier.md. Two reasons, both worth a follow-up ticket if the owner wants the registry branch instead: (a) the issue states agent types are read once at session start, and whether a SessionStart hook's write to ~/.claude/agents/ lands before that read is unverified - it can only be settled by an experiment in a real container, which this worktree cannot run; (b) proving it would need a live-tree write, which is a hard rail here.
- .claude/hooks/session-start.sh installs only the plugin's skills tree into ~/.claude/skills/ - it never copies claude/agents/ anywhere the container's agent registry reads. So no cloud session has ANY custom agent type, not just fleet-verifier. Anything else that pins `agentType` to a dotfiles-defined agent (now or later) breaks in cloud the same way issue 316 describes. Out of scope here; a bootstrap-side ticket would cover all such agents at once.
- tools/ticket-fleet-branch.test.js's driveCodeLane harness evaluates the fleet's code lane with its free bindings supplied as AsyncFunction parameters, so every new top-level binding the lane body references (here `verifierAgentType`) silently breaks that test until the parameter list is extended. It fails with a bare ReferenceError that names the binding but not the cause. A comment at the wrapper, or deriving the parameter list from the body, would make the coupling visible; I extended the list rather than changing the mechanism.
- Ambiguity read, issue 318 criterion 3: "resolves hunks whose every line is one of the four stamp keys and leaves any other hunk untouched with a non-zero exit" is implemented literally in /home/user/claude-dotfiles/.claude/worktrees/wf_f862c7a0-27e-23/tools/resolve-stamp-conflict.js - qualifying hunks are rewritten in place to the chosen side, every other hunk survives byte-for-byte with its markers, and the process exits 1 if any survived. The alternative reading (write nothing at all unless every hunk qualifies) was not built. A conflict side that is empty is deliberately NOT treated as stamp-only: a deleted stamp block is not a stamp rotation.
- The two regeneration commands are claude-dotfiles-specific, so the fleet's new `regenCommands` arg defaults to null, which tells the deliver agent to read them out of CLAUDE.md rather than baking `python3 tools/build-cloud-plugin.py --from-mirror --home 'C:\Users\Dan'` into a plugin script that is served to every repo. A fork with different tooling passes its own list. If a repo's CLAUDE.md ever stops naming those commands plainly, the deliver stage will merge and then fail its post-merge test run instead of regenerating - worth a claims.json pin on that CLAUDE.md section, which this ticket did not cover.
- `node tools/resolve-stamp-conflict.js` is a claude-dotfiles path, but aac-skills/ticket-fleet/ticket-fleet.js is served by the plugin to every repo. The deliver prompt handles the gap by telling the agent to treat the SKILL.md stamp class as an unresolvable conflict where that script is absent, so other repos degrade to 'block and report' rather than guessing. A repo that wants the stamp class has to vendor the script itself; there is no mechanism (harness template, plugin-relative path) that copies it, and adding one was out of scope here.
- Behavioral note verified against real `git merge` output: when a branch changed BOTH a SKILL.md's stamp block and its prose, git emits a single conflict hunk spanning both, not two hunks. The resolver refuses that hunk (exit 1) and the deliver stage reclassifies the file as a real merge - which is exactly the PR #306 failure mode the ticket describes. A resolver that scanned only for the four key names without requiring every line of the hunk to be one of them would silently take master's side and drop the prose edit again.
- /home/user/claude-dotfiles/.claude/worktrees/wf_f862c7a0-27e-25/agents/skills/triage/SKILL.md is the repo mirror of the live ~/.claude/skills/triage/SKILL.md, which is a read-only production path from a cloud container. This branch edits the mirror only (the documented cloud path: skill-stamps stamp + build-cloud-plugin --from-mirror were run). A desktop session must reconcile the live tree after this merges - the freshness loop's origin-ahead auto-install, or sync.ps1 -Mode pull - or the next sync push will overwrite the mirror with the unedited live copy and silently drop the new 'Triaging a discovery list into tickets' section.
- agents/skills/project-harness/templates/tracker-audit.js is a second, already-divergent copy of tools/tracker-audit.js that the project-harness skill installs into every other repo (it carries a longer GH-CALLS header and different comment wording than the repo copy on master). The new advisory duplicate-title? check landed only in tools/tracker-audit.js, so harnessed repos do not get it. Porting it needs a surgical patch to the template plus a project-harness version bump and an upgrade-table row (step 8, 're-copy tracker-audit.js'); out of scope for issue 319, which names tools/tracker-audit.js only.
- tools/ticket-fleet-branch.test.js drives the fleet's code lane by extracting the text between the [FLEET-CODE-LANE-START/END] markers and evaluating it with every free binding passed by name into an AsyncFunction wrapper (agent, log, cfg, runId, scout, PR_CHECK, IMPL, VERDICT, DELIVERED, instrument, rules). Any new module-scope helper the lane references therefore fails the test with 'X is not defined' until the wrapper's parameter list is extended - it happened here for dedupeBrief. Worth a comment at the extractor, or a wrapper that takes a bindings object, so the next fleet edit does not read the failure as a real regression.
- Ambiguity in issue 319's second acceptance criterion (script-side chaining OR a SKILL.md instruction to run the chores in separate waves): implemented the script side - the scout now returns a required per-ticket discoveryTriage boolean, and the wave runner puts every flagged chore in one sequential lane while all other tickets still run in parallel - and documented that behaviour in aac-skills/ticket-fleet/SKILL.md rather than building both readings. Callers of the fleet that pin the SCOUT schema shape elsewhere (a fork such as aac-routines' or aac-cockpit's edited .claude/workflows/ticket-fleet.js copy) will not have the new field until they re-copy the plugin source.

## Run (ticket-fleet)

- Issue 120's `writing` acceptance box has no skill behind it. Commit 518e63a (2026-09-14, issue 178) deleted aac-skills/writing/ and aac-skills/counterparty-redline/ plus their packaged copies, on the owner's instruction to 'remove writing, writing-dan and counterparty-redline from aac-skills so the skill list stops offering them'. aac-house-writing-standard is the surviving AAC writing skill and is not one of the ticket's fifteen. The box cannot be ticked by doing the work; it needs the owner to strike it, retarget it at aac-house-writing-standard, or close it as overtaken by 178. Recorded in docs/tickets/120-decision.md.
- Six of the fifteen skills (aac-google-access, claude-md-lint, consistency-audit, consolidate-memory, maintain-repo, update-cloud-plugin) are real directories in ~/.claude/skills whose only repo copy is the generated mirror claude/skills/. A cloud session cannot land them: CLAUDE.md's carve-out covers aac-skills/ and agents/skills/ only, the live tree is a read-only production path under this agent's rails, and hand-writing the mirror is the failure the previous attempt was rejected for. Their rewrites are committed as complete SKILL.md files under docs/tickets/120-personal-skills/ with a README carrying the copy-and-push procedure. LANDING THEM NEEDS A DESKTOP SESSION on Dan's machine: copy the six files over ~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md, run sync.ps1 -Mode push (which rotates their stamps and rebuilds the payload), then delete the staging folder. Two files carry mirror home-path tokens (__USERHOME_FWD__ in aac-google-access, __USERHOME__ in update-cloud-plugin) that only sync.ps1 -Mode pull expands.
- ASSUMPTION: read the ticket's 'reference material that only some runs need disclosed behind a pointer' as licence to move project-harness's nineteen-row upgrade table out of SKILL.md into a sibling UPGRADES.md (only the upgrade branch reaches it, and only the rows above a repo's marker). The rows travel verbatim but were re-sorted into version order and joined into one table: they were interleaved 8-before-7 with blank lines between rows, which Markdown renders as a dozen separate tables. session-check/harness-version.js's '**Current version: 19.**' regex target stays in SKILL.md and its 13 tests pass. No other skill got structural disclosure; the rest of the pass is prose-level.
- tests/restore-test.ps1 cannot run in this container: no pwsh on PATH and core.hooksPath is unset, so .githooks/pre-commit never fired on any of these commits. The acceptance item 'restore test green on every commit' is unverified here. The change surface is documentation plus one new UPGRADES.md file inside an existing skill folder, and the only executable contract in range (harness-version.js reading the Current version line) is tested green, but someone with PowerShell should run `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tests\restore-test.ps1 -From worktree` before merge.
- The cloud-plugin sweep 'reports current after the last push' criterion is a post-push state that no cloud worktree can reach: the sweep reads ~/.claude/skills and stamps ~/.claude/hook-state. The payload in this branch is rebuilt and reproducible (CI's own diff check passes), so the sweep stamps itself on the next sync.ps1 -Mode push from the main checkout.
- FOLLOW-UPS.md on master already carries a discovery block from an earlier issue-120 attempt claiming all 15 skills passed the lens with commits 2fc568c/8675994/bdf0ed1/0327175 — none of which exist in this repository's object store. Those discoveries were merged without the commits they cite, so the file now records work that is not on master. Worth a pass over FOLLOW-UPS.md to strike or annotate that block, and worth checking whether the fleet's discovery-collection step can land discoveries from a branch whose commits never merged.
- aac-skills/writing was deleted in 518e63a but the 'writing' box in issue 120's body was never updated, and issue 120 predates 178. tools/tracker-audit.js has a stale-premise advisory class for exactly this shape (a ticket citing state a later closed issue changed); it does not appear to catch an acceptance box whose subject was deleted by another ticket. A check for 'acceptance box names a path that no longer exists' would have caught this before two attempts spent a cycle on it.
- Implemented the FIRST branch of AC2 (targeted insertion), not the second (prove the round-trip safe on 5.1 + pwsh 7). Reason: this container has no PowerShell at all - neither `powershell` nor `pwsh` is installed, and the worktree-isolation guard on the Bash tool refuses to execute a downloaded pwsh binary ('this command runs pwsh in a plain command ... Refusing to run it'), so the both-engines proof was not obtainable here. The surgical-insertion branch makes engine-specific JSON serialization behaviour irrelevant, and the finding is recorded in the script header.
- Nothing written in PowerShell on this branch could be EXECUTED here, including tests/restore-test.ps1 and the new tests/settings-invariants.tests.ps1 (same no-PowerShell rail). To compensate I ported the insertion algorithm (Find-ObjectBrace / New-HeadInsertion / edit application) line-for-line to Python and ran it over 8 fixtures - representative file with one record missing, false-flag flip, flag absent from an existing record, empty `projects` object, no `projects` key, minified single-line, empty `{}` document, CRLF - all produced valid JSON with exactly the intended change, and the representative case was a single contiguous insertion. I also ran a bracket/here-string/quote balance lint over the .ps1 files (it agrees with the known-good suites). A Windows runner (the pre-commit hook or CI) is still needed to confirm the .ps1 files themselves run.
- tools/settings-invariants.ps1's settings.json half (Set-SettingsInvariant) still throws when a `permissions` block exists with other keys but no `defaultMode`, telling the operator to add the key by hand. The new New-HeadInsertion helper added for the -Trust half would solve exactly that case surgically, but the ticket is scoped to the ~/.claude.json half so I did not touch it. Worth a follow-up ticket.
- Behaviour narrowing worth knowing: the -Trust path now locates an EXISTING record by text search for its JSON key literal (minimal escaping, matching Node's JSON.stringify). If a machine's ~/.claude.json spells a key differently - e.g. a non-ASCII home path Node wrote as a \uXXXX escape - the search misses and the tool throws 'refuses to guess' instead of editing, where the old full round-trip would have handled any spelling. The throw is safe (nothing is written, the file is untouched) and the message names the manual fix, but it is a case the old code silently handled.
- sync.ps1 invokes tools/settings-invariants.ps1 via `& powershell @args_ | ForEach-Object { Write-Host ... }` and never reads $LASTEXITCODE, so a throw from the invariant tool prints into the pull's output and the pull carries on reporting success. Pre-existing, unchanged by this ticket, but it means the new 'refusing to write, the edit did not verify' guard is advisory rather than fatal to a pull.
- Stamp lineage drifts under the documented two-command flow in CLAUDE.md. Running `python3 tools/skill-stamps.py stamp aac-skills agents/skills` and then `python3 tools/build-cloud-plugin.py --from-mirror --home 'C:\Users\Dan'` rotates a changed skill's stamp TWICE: the stamper writes rev N+1 with the correct `previous-modified`, then the packager rewrites the file (local-path retargeting) and rotates again to rev N+2, whose `previous-modified` is the stamper's timestamp from seconds earlier rather than the last published version. Observed on aac-skills/ticket-fleet/SKILL.md: rev 8 -> 9 -> 10 with previous-modified 2026-09-16T03:29:01Z instead of 2026-09-15T22:39:49Z. Running the packager alone produces the correct single rotation (rev 9, previous 2026-09-15T22:39:49Z), which is what this branch carries. `skill-stamps.py check` passes either way, so nothing catches it. Either CLAUDE.md should stop naming both commands for aac-skills edits, or the packager should not re-rotate a stamp whose recorded `modified` already matches the current content hash.
- Assumption taken where issue 317 is silent: `priorImpl` / `priorProbe` entries are keyed by ticket number and consumed as-is with no schema validation. A malformed or partial entry is not rejected up front - it falls through the lane's existing failure branch (`!impl || !impl.committed` for code, `!probe || !probe.items.length` for probe), which records a failed attempt 1 and lets attempt 2 run the stage normally. Entries are also read only when `attempt === 1`, per the criterion's wording.
- The probe lane's delivery stage read `probe.blocked.length` unguarded, so any PROBE result lacking a `blocked` array threw inside the deliver branch. Reuse makes that reachable (a journal-lifted entry may omit the key), so this change guards it with `probe.blocked || []`. The same unguarded-field pattern is not present elsewhere in the lanes, but nothing validates a structured-output result against its declared schema before the script indexes into it.
- `testCommand` is now a free binding referenced inside the FLEET-CODE-LANE marker block (replacing two `scout.testCommand` reads). Anything that extracts that block and evaluates it - today only `driveCodeLane` in tools/ticket-fleet-branch.test.js, which was updated - must supply it as a wrapper parameter or the extracted lane throws ReferenceError.
- Issue 320's "84 bullets" is a diff-line count, not a bullet count. Commit 5160c68 (wave 2) inserted 34 lines holding 30 bullets and commit f0e8a5d (wave 3) inserted 44 lines holding 41 bullets, so the real total is 71. The comment on #320 that widened scope also said wave 3 was "50 bullets". Any future triage chore whose acceptance criterion counts bullets should count `^- ` lines in the commit, not `git show --stat` insertions, or the criterion can never be satisfied as written.
- The wave-2 and wave-3 discovery commits are NOT on master. 5160c68 and f0e8a5d live only on `claude/affectionate-maxwell-gqp5vw`, the branch of open PR #276 ("chore: mirror the plugin ticket-fleet script into .claude/workflows" — an unrelated title that happens to carry them). FOLLOW-UPS.md on master has never held those 71 bullets, so a master-based worktree following the ticket literally finds nothing to triage. The fleet's report writer appends discoveries to whatever branch it is on rather than to master, so every run whose report branch does not merge leaves its discoveries invisible to the triage chore that is filed for them. Worth a fleet-side fix: append discoveries on a branch of their own, or have the report writer name the commit sha in the chore ticket it files.
- The Edit tool silently stripped the single CR byte on FOLLOW-UPS.md line 234 while editing a different line. That file is LF throughout except its last line, which is CRLF; the repo pins `* -text` so bytes are canonical, and the strip showed up as a phantom `1` deletion in `git diff --numstat`. It had to be restored with a byte-level python rewrite. This is the same hazard the wave-3 bullet W3-16 recorded for a Python rewrite of a CRLF mirror file, now confirmed for the harness's own Edit tool on a mixed-ending file. Anything editing a file under `claude/`, `codex/`, `memory/` or any other byte-pinned path in this repo should diff `--numstat` afterwards and expect pure addition.
- `tools/tracker-audit.js`'s `proseBlockers()` matches the `## Blocked by` section to the END of the body (`(?=^##+\s|$(?![\s\S]))`), so any provenance or attribution footer placed after that heading is parsed as a prose blocker. Two tickets filed here (#335, #338) went red with [ungated-dependency] purely because their footer read "Filed from the run 6aa9c56e discovery triage (#320)." The repo's own issue template puts `## Blocked by` last, so every agent-filed ticket that adds a footer will trip this. A `None`-prefixed section short-circuits, which is why the other six new tickets were clean by luck. Fix candidates: stop the section at a blank-line-separated non-list paragraph, or ignore `#N` that appears after the last list item.
- `gh issue view`, `gh issue list` and every other gh path that routes through GitHub GraphQL returns HTTP 403 in this container ("GitHub GraphQL is not available from Claude Code sessions; use the REST API"). `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/...` works for reads AND writes — issue create, issue comment, issue PATCH and label PATCH all succeeded. The visible casualty is `tools/tracker-audit.js`: its board-says-done and not-on-board checks sit on ProjectsV2 GraphQL and are skipped in cloud, which it reports as a NOTE rather than a failure. This corroborates wave-2 bullet W2-16 and is now carried on #212 and #322.
- Issue 323's body quotes run_ledger flags that the shipped CLI does not accept: it says `queue ... --items <items.json>` and `record --routine todoist-triage --from -`, but surreptakos/aac-routines src/aac_routines/run_ledger.py build_parser() defines `queue --surfaced <path> [--snapshot] [--config config/task-capture.json] [--dismissals]` and `record --input <path>` (no --routine on record, no stdin form; --input is read via Path(...).read_text). I wrote the skill against the real CLI and against aac-routines routines/todoist-triage.md, which agrees with the code. If the ticket author intended `--items`/`--from -`, the aac-routines CLI is what needs changing, not this skill.
- Issue 323 says the drop-in paragraphs are quoted in aac-routines docs/runbooks/run-ledger.md 'under "What each routine calls"'. That heading does not exist in the file on main; the equivalent content is under '## Commands' and '## Where each routine is told to do this', and the full prose the skill needed is in aac-routines routines/todoist-triage.md. I sourced the wording from those two plus the run_ledger.py source.
- The skill's step 6 vocabulary rule (plain English, no internal names in what Dan reads) now sits next to shell commands and JSON field names in the same step. I added an explicit sentence that the commands and their JSON are plumbing rather than report text, but if the skill grows more machinery in step 6 it may be worth splitting the report contract from the ledger bookkeeping into separate steps.
- The skill names the aac-routines checkout only as 'the `Meta/aac-routines` project', not an absolute path, because this repo's rail forbids hardcoded home paths in synced/packaged files and the packager folds the home path out of every content hash. If a triage run needs an exact path, the right home for it is a templated value, not skill prose.
- tools/build-cloud-plugin.py stamps a fresh timestamp version into .claude-plugin/marketplace.json and marketplace/aac-skills/.claude-plugin/plugin.json on every run, so any rebuild dirties those two files even when nothing else changed. CI (skill-stamps.yml) diffs only the skills tree and hooks.json, so this is harmless in CI, but it makes 'rebuild and check git status' a misleading local check; I reverted the second rebuild's version bump to keep the tree clean.

## Run (ticket-fleet)

- Cross-repo work this worktree cannot do (hard rail: no writes outside the worktree, no pushes): surreptakos/aac-routines `.claude/workflows/ticket-fleet.js` is a pre-contract fork - fetched read-only via `gh api`, it takes no `runId` arg at all (it predates PR #55), and `node tools/ticket-fleet-contract.js <copy>` reports `STALE ... contract unmarked (pre-v2 fork) vs plugin v2`, exit 1. Refreshing it needs a commit in that repo: re-copy the plugin script over the fork, re-apply its Setup/auth phase, and pass `contractVersion: 2` + a fresh `invocationId` wherever its runbook launches the fleet.
- surreptakos/aac-cockpit is not reachable from this session at all - `gh api` returns "GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session" and `add_repo` returns "you don't have access to surreptakos/aac-cockpit". Its fork's contract state is therefore unverified; someone with access must run the same fork audit and refresh before the next fleet run there.
- The behaviors behind the two new SCOUT fields live on unmerged sibling branches, and this change ships only the fields and the scout prompt that produce them: confining the wave to `candidateNumbers` is PR #325 (issue 298) and chaining `discoveryTriage` chores so two do not file the same finding twice is PR #329 (issue 319). Expect a textual conflict in the SCOUT schema and the scout prompt when either merges; the resolution is to keep both (the field text here was copied verbatim from those branches to make that trivial).
- `invocationId` and its splice into the open-PR guard's prompt and label were ported verbatim from PR #312 (issue 291), which is still open. Once this lands, issue 291 will read as already fixed on master and its PR will conflict only in the surrounding comment block.
- `agents/skills/project-harness/SKILL.md` is the repo mirror of `~/.claude/skills/project-harness/SKILL.md`; step 15's launch instruction was updated here on the branch, but the live tree still tells a session to pass `args.runId` alone. A desktop session must make the same edit in `~/.claude` and run `sync.ps1 -Mode push`, or the next push overwrites the mirror back to the v1 wording.
- The generated `memory/` mirror still describes the v1 launch: `workflow-runtime-quirks.md` ("scripts now require args.runId (PR #55); mint it with printf %x $(date +%s)") and the MEMORY.md index line will read stale after this merges. `memory/` is generated and must not be hand-edited here; the fix is a live-tree memory edit plus a sync push from the desktop.
- Assumption recorded for issue 334: the ticket names `~/.claude/projects/*/tool-results/*.txt`, but the exclusion I shipped is the whole `*/.claude/projects/*` subtree. Every file under ~/.claude/projects is harness-written during a run - the session transcript `<session-id>.jsonl`, `tool-results/*.txt`, `subagents/` and `workflows/` - so an exclusion narrowed to tool-results would still have reported the transcript and subagent logs as a live-tree breach. I implemented the exclusion (not the allowlist) alternative the acceptance criteria offer, because it is a one-token change to the existing find and keeps every non-harness path under ~/.claude, ~/.codex and ~/.agents reportable, where an allowlist of four named paths would silently stop reporting writes to e.g. ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md or ~/.claude/agents.
- Pre-existing, not fixed (out of scope for issue 334): the verifier's live-tree sweep runs `find ~/.claude ~/.codex ~/.agents ...`, but `~/.agents` does not exist in a cloud container - confirmed here, `find /root/.claude /root/.codex /root/.agents -maxdepth 0` prints `find: '/root/.agents': No such file or directory` to stderr and exits 1. A verifier that treats a non-zero exit as 'the check could not be run' may report the rail as unverifiable rather than clean. A `-d` existence filter or `2>/dev/null` on that find would settle it.
- The `duplicate-title?` advisory that issue 336's AC treats as already living in `tools/tracker-audit.js` was NOT on `origin/master` at branch time. It exists only on the unmerged sibling fleet branch `agent/issue-319-attempt1-wf_6aa9c56e-w11`, commit 37b9d2527b8f9f3d53839923afeaca24d9027466. Because the template is now generated from the repo copy, the check had to be in the source for the template to carry it, so I applied that commit's `tools/tracker-audit.js` and `tools/tracker-audit.test.js` hunks verbatim (via `git apply --3way`) onto this branch. The added text is byte-identical to #319's, so a three-way merge of both branches should resolve without conflict — but if #319's PR is amended or rebased before it merges, the two copies will collide and whoever merges second must keep one copy of `normalizeTitle` / `duplicateTitleFindings` / the `duplicate-title?` report block / the `ORDER` entry.
- The pre-v20 harness template exported DIFFERENT names and a different return shape from the repo copy, and the generated v20 template silently renames them: `restIssueToShape` -> `normalizeIssue`, `restPrToShape` -> `normalizePr`, `filterIssuesOnly` -> `issuesOnly`, and `parseGithubSlug` now returns `{owner, name}` where the template returned `{owner, repo}`. Any harnessed repo that wrote its own unit test or local extension against the old template's exports will break the moment someone performs the v20 step-8 re-copy. The v20 upgrade row says to diff first on repos with local extensions, but nobody has audited the eight harnessed repos for callers of those four names — that audit is not part of this ticket.
- `agents/skills/project-harness/templates/tracker-audit.js` lives inside the generated `agents/` mirror, and the generator (`tools/build-harness-tracker-audit.js`) writes only the mirror — the live `~/.claude/skills/project-harness/templates/tracker-audit.js` was left untouched per the live-tree hard rail. Until someone runs `sync.ps1 -Mode pull` on the desktop after this merges, the live tree holds the OLD hand-maintained template, and a `sync.ps1 -Mode push` performed before that pull will overwrite the generated file in the repo with the stale live copy (the failure `tools/tracker-audit-template.test.js` now catches, but only after the push is already committed). Consider teaching `tools/build-cloud-plugin.py` (which every push runs) to invoke the generator, so push cannot re-introduce the drift; that is beyond this ticket's scope.
- Bumping the harness marker to 20 records that older installs are behind; it does not propagate anything. Eight harnessed repos still carry the pre-v20 `tools/tracker-audit.js` (no citation narrowing, no `duplicate-title?`) and each needs the v20 step-8 re-copy. `docs/agents/harness-version.md` in claude-dotfiles itself was bumped to 20 because this repo is the upstream of the v20 file (its `tools/tracker-audit.js` IS the v20 content), which also keeps the `/session-start` behind-version STOP from firing on this repo.
- Hard-rail consequence, unresolved and needing an owner-machine action: tools/claude-md-lint.js is now ahead of its generated copies claude/skills/claude-md-lint/claude-md-lint.js and marketplace/aac-skills/skills/claude-md-lint/claude-md-lint.js, plus both SKILL.md files (they document the old 'Exit 0 clean, 1 findings' contract and do not mention --warn-only). Only an owner-machine edit of ~/.claude/skills/claude-md-lint/* followed by sync.ps1 -Mode push can refresh them; a worktree cannot touch ~/.claude or claude/. Until that push, cloud and other-repo sessions running the skill copy still get the old exit code.
- The byte-identity test in tools/claude-md-lint.test.js made tools/claude-md-lint.js effectively uneditable from any session without a live ~/.claude tree: the only ways to make it pass were hand-editing the generated mirror (the one rule) or a Windows sync push. I changed the test to allow a difference only while tools/ declares a higher MIRROR_REVISION than the mirror (a bump that shows in the diff and is cleared by the next push), and to keep failing on an undeclared byte difference. The same trap exists for any other tools/ file with a generated skill twin; a general fix would be moving the claude-md-lint skill out of the generated claude/ mirror into hand-written aac-skills/, the way issue 138 collapsed the ticket-fleet copies.
- tests/restore-test.ps1 still carries its own copies of the warn-only lists ($mdLintWarnRoot / $mdLintWarnMirror) and parses findings rather than reading the exit code, so the policy now lives in two places and can drift again. I left it alone deliberately: no PowerShell exists in this container (pwsh/powershell both absent), so any edit there would ship unverified. Once someone can run the suite on Windows, Test-MdLintGate can collapse to 'run the linter, check the exit code, print the findings it emitted'.
- Ambiguity resolved: the ticket offered a flag, a per-file default or a config block. Criterion 1 requires a bare `node tools/claude-md-lint.js claude/CLAUDE.md` to exit 0, and claude/CLAUDE.md is a generated byte mirror that cannot carry an in-file directive, so I implemented a per-file default table keyed on path suffix (claude/CLAUDE.md -> size, volatile, code-derivable, tutorial; every other file -> size) plus a --warn-only override. No in-file `claude-md-lint-warn:` directive was added.
- Adding the new sentence to the linter bullet in the root CLAUDE.md pushed that bullet's wrapped continuation block to 124 words and tripped the linter's own `tutorial` rule (threshold 120), briefly turning CLAUDE.md red. I compressed the surrounding sentences back under the threshold. Worth knowing: the tutorial rule counts a list item's wrapped continuation lines as a standalone paragraph, so any addition to a long bullet in this file can go red without the bullet itself being prose.
- Issue 339's acceptance criterion 2 is disjunctive and I took route 2 (document the limitation) rather than route 1 (install claude/agents/ from the bootstrap). Reason, stated as the assumption: the experiment shows a SessionStart write is visible only from the NEXT session in the same container, so installing the tree would deliver a capability that works from session 2 onward and fails on session 1 - exactly the 'looks available and silently is not' state the ticket was filed against. If the owner later wants the agents present anyway (e.g. for long-lived containers that host several sessions), that is a separate ticket and would need the bootstrap marker to say which sessions can rely on it.
- The container this ran in already had ~/.claude/agents/fleet-verifier.md on disk (mtime 2026-09-15 23:27) although the bootstrap hook never installs it - some earlier session wrote it by hand as the #316/#322 workaround. That file is invisible to the session that wrote it but live for every later session in the same container, which is the half-working state the decision avoids. The fleet is safe against it: pickVerifierAgent returns null whenever remote is true, regardless of the file. Worth a sweep for other hand-placed files under ~/.claude in long-lived containers.
- FOLLOW-UPS.md line 128 records the now-settled discovery 'The Workflow runtime's exact policy on `process` is unverified from a static test ... Callers who know the container shape can pass instrument: gh or mcp to bypass the sniff.' That entry is stale after this branch: the script no longer reads process.env at all. FOLLOW-UPS.md is append-only by convention so I did not edit it, but the entry now contradicts the shipped script.
- The env-probe adds one extra (Haiku-tier, effort:low) agent call to every fleet run, and its behaviour under the Workflow tool's resume cache is untested - no test here can drive a live Workflow invocation. The first real fleet run from both a desktop and a cloud session is what would confirm the switch resolves correctly end to end and that a resumed run replays the probe from cache rather than re-measuring into a different answer.
- Running the experiment needed a nested `claude -p` in this container. The arms themselves used CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR into the scratchpad so no governance file under ~/.claude was written, but Claude Code itself wrote session-transcript artefacts there for my own session and for the one smoke-test invocation whose cwd was the scratchpad (~/.claude/projects/-tmp-claude-0-...-exp339/<id>.jsonl, plus ~/.claude/sessions/, policy-limits.json, remote-settings.json). A verifier's `find ~/.claude ~/.codex ~/.agents -newermt` hard-rail check will list those; none is an authored file and no skill, agent, setting or memory file was touched.
- `gh api --method POST .../issues/339/comments` was refused by the auto-mode classifier ([External System Writes]) in this container; the evidence comment landed via mcp__github__add_issue_comment instead (https://github.com/surreptakos/claude-dotfiles/issues/339#issuecomment-5692607542). Any repo doc or script that tells a cloud session to post comments with the `gh` REST path should name the MCP tool as the working path there - the ticket-fleet script's own gh-instrument commentPost() rule would hit the same denial.
- Implemented the ticket's preferred option (derive bindings from the body, no test edit needed for a new lane binding) rather than the comment-only fallback: /home/user/claude-dotfiles/.claude/worktrees/wf_efe925db-5a2-15/tools/ticket-fleet-branch.test.js now evaluates the extracted FLEET-CODE-LANE body inside `with (scope)` over a Proxy whose `has` trap claims every name — harness stubs resolve to the stub, real globals to themselves, anything unmodelled to an inert permissive stub. Function-constructor bodies are non-strict regardless of the module's 'use strict', so `with` is legal there. Verified against the real fleet script by temporarily inserting `const brief = dedupeBrief(t) + newVerifierAgentType` into the lane body: all tests still passed with no harness edit, then the insertion was reverted.
- Non-obvious trap worth knowing if this harness is touched again: the scope Proxy's `get` trap must return undefined for symbol keys. `with` reads Symbol.unscopables off the scope object, and answering it with the truthy permissive stub marks every name unscopable, which reintroduces exactly the `ReferenceError: X is not defined` the ticket is about.
- Consequence of the permissive fallback, accepted deliberately and not in scope here: a future lane binding whose value actually changes control flow (e.g. a new boolean guard) will silently resolve to a truthy inert stub rather than erroring, so it would surface as a call-order assertion failure instead of a ReferenceError. The comment block above `laneScope` states this so the next reader knows where to look.
- Pre-existing test failures, not caused by this change: `agents/skills/session-check/check.test.js` tests 5 ('cloud fallback: outside a cloud container, a missing interpreter still STOPs') and 17 ('cloud bootstrap: the whole section is silent on a local (non-cloud) session') fail whenever the suite itself runs inside a Claude cloud container, because they assume the ambient env has no CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE_* and runChecker inherits process.env. Verified against the unmodified HEAD copies of check.js/check.test.js run from a scratch dir: the same two fail there. The fix is the one I used for the new desktop-side test — pass `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE_SESSION_ID: ''` and `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE_ENVIRONMENT_TYPE: ''` in those two tests' env — but it is outside issue 345's scope so I left them alone.
- Nothing on Linux runs `agents/skills/session-check/check.test.js`. The repo's configured test command (`.claude/session.json`) is `powershell ... tests/restore-test.ps1 -From worktree && node --test tests/docs-claims.test.js tools/*.test.js`, and no GitHub workflow runs it either; the Windows restore test only executes the restored `check.js` once and runs `identity.test.js`. So the new `host:` unit tests are real and passing but are not gated by CI — a future regression in the host predicate would not go red. Worth a ticket to either add `agents/skills/*/**.test.js` to a CI job or to the restore suite's check 9 block.
- The live source of session-check is `~/.claude/skills/session-check/` (read-only production path from this worktree), so this branch edits only the repo mirror at `agents/skills/session-check/` plus the regenerated `marketplace/` payload, per CLAUDE.md's cloud-session path. The desktop's live copy only gains the `host` predicate when someone runs `sync.ps1 -Mode pull` on that machine; until then the desktop half of the feature exists in the repo and the plugin but not in `~/.claude`.
- Ambiguity I resolved toward the literal wording of the ticket ('skips a check whose host does not match'): the predicate is a plain `c.host && c.host !== HOST`, where `HOST` is `'cloud'` in a container and `'desktop'` otherwise. That means an unrecognised or misspelled value (`"Desktop"`, `"local"`) matches no host and silently skips the check on every machine forever. I deliberately did not add validation or a warn-on-unknown-host path; if that silent-disable hazard matters, it is a small follow-up in the same loop.
- Fourth acceptance criterion ('a follow-up in aac-routines sets host: desktop on the board-sweep entry') is in a different repository and is filed from there once this lands — nothing in this repo to do for it. The consumer-side shape it needs is: `{ "name": "board sweep", "run": "...", "host": "desktop" }` in that repo's `.claude/session.json` `checks` array.
- Portability assumption I had to make and built for: `tools/orchestrator-tree-guard.js` ships in surreptakos/aac-routines only, but `aac-skills/ticket-fleet/ticket-fleet.js` is the one script served to every fleeted repo. A straight port of aac-routines issue 192's baseline would have aborted every bill-intake / contract-builder / sales-cockpit / zoho-source-of-truth fleet run before Scout. So the ported guard has a third mode: `cfg.treeGuard` defaults to `'auto'`, the baseline command is prefixed with `[ -f <treeGuardScript> ] || exit 3`, and exit 3 turns the guard off with a log line under 'auto' while `treeGuard: true` makes the same absence a hard abort. `cfg.treeGuardScript` is overridable for a repo that keeps the tool elsewhere. The ticket did not name this case; the alternative reading (port the guard unconditionally) would break four repos.
- The other three repos served by the orchestrator Routines (bill-intake, contract-builder, sales-cockpit, zoho-source-of-truth) have neither `tools/orchestrator-tree-guard.js` nor `tools/fleet-run-record.js`, so both aac-routines mechanisms are no-ops there: the guard disables itself, and the run-record log line names a command those repos cannot run (RUNBOOK step 4 says to note that in the heartbeat and move on). If the orchestrator's isolation and forensics are meant to hold for every fleeted repo, those two tools need porting into each served repo, or into this repo as a plugin-served pair the fleet can point at. Out of scope here — this ticket ports the fleet script, not the tools it calls.
- The tree-guard checkpoints ported from aac-routines cover the code lane only, matching the fork, which has no probe or human lane. The probe and human lanes' verifier and deliver sub-agents also run unisolated in the orchestrator's checkout; they are covered only by the `pre-report` checkpoint, which runs after every ticket association has been dropped, so a leak from those lanes is attributed by content or to ticket #0. Adding per-lane checkpoints there would be a genuine extension of aac-routines 192/270 rather than a port.
- `aac-skills/ticket-fleet/ticket-fleet.js` now runs one extra cheap agent (`tree-guard:baseline`, haiku, effort low) on every invocation in every repo, including repos where the probe immediately turns the guard off. A presence check the workflow runtime could make without a sub-session (it has no `fs`) would remove that cost; none exists today.
- Ambiguity resolved: `config/task-capture.json` and its `inbox_project_id` key do not exist anywhere in claude-dotfiles (they live in aac-routines, per the blocker surreptakos/aac-routines#304). The skill therefore names the Inbox project by that config key and repo rather than hard-coding a project id the way Current Work (6JHrw6mXXgrrWqxG) and the backlog (6XMPVX96VgH6vwHR) are hard-coded. If the intent was a literal id in the skill, it has to come from the aac-routines config once #304 lands.
- Out of scope, left alone: the 'Filters (exist in Todoist, favorited)' section of aac-skills/todoist-triage/SKILL.md still documents the Triage queue filter as 'both projects minus every ball label, `no-sweep`, and `merged`'. Now that the read step covers three projects, that saved Todoist filter is narrower than the run. Editing the text would claim a change to a live Todoist filter that nobody made, so the filter itself needs updating in Todoist and the line re-synced afterwards.
- Cross-repo coupling worth a check at delivery: this skill now asserts that the aac-routines router is the only thing that moves an item out of the Inbox, and step 5 forbids a `projectId` into or out of the Inbox. That contract is only true once surreptakos/aac-routines#304 (router core) and #307 (routine step) land; until then the Inbox is read and ruled on in place but nothing promotes routed items.
- tools/build-cloud-plugin.py writes dist/aac-skills.zip (3089 KB) as a side effect of `--from-mirror`. It is gitignored, so it does not pollute the branch, but a CI runner short on disk should know the rebuild step materializes a 3 MB zip.

## Run (ticket-fleet)

- Assumption taken on the ticket's wording: "the last two `## Run (ticket-fleet)` headings of FOLLOW-UPS.md" does not resolve on master, whose last two sections are from run 6aa9c553. The 65 bullets are commits b2ce4cd (wave 4, 26 bullets) and d0b97b5 (wave 5, 39) on the unmerged branch claude/affectionate-maxwell-gqp5vw (PR #276); the 26/39 split confirms the reading. The ledger therefore keys bullets by commit + position, exactly as the waves 2/3 triage (aae7e6b) did. That the discoveries are unreachable from master at all is now filed as issue 360.
- Pre-existing tracker drift, untouched: `node tools/tracker-audit.js` exits 1 because issue #322's body says "blocked by #316" with no native dependency edge. The audit prints the exact fix: `gh api --method POST repos/surreptakos/claude-dotfiles/issues/322/dependencies/blocked_by -F issue_id=$(gh api repos/surreptakos/claude-dotfiles/issues/316 --jq .id)`. One command, nobody's ticket.
- The audit raises a `stale-premise?` advisory against issue #358 itself: its body cites closed #281 (the duplicate pair it warns future triage agents about) with no wording acknowledging it is settled. Every discovery-triage chore that names a closed duplicate as a cautionary example will trip this, so the advisory class and the chore template are in tension — worth a rule that a chore body may cite a closed issue as an example.
- From a cloud container the ProjectsV2 GraphQL endpoint is unavailable, so tracker-audit's board-says-done and not-on-board checks are skipped and reported as a NOTE. A container can therefore never produce a complete audit verdict, only a partial one — relevant to any acceptance criterion that reads "tracker-audit is clean" rather than "reaches a verdict".
- gh REST through the session proxy handled every tracker write this task needed — issue create with labels, issue comment, and POST /issues/{n}/dependencies/blocked_by (used to gate #366 on #347) — while every GraphQL-backed gh subcommand (`gh issue view`, `gh issue list`) returns HTTP 403. Wave-5 bullet W5-23 reported a gh REST comment POST being refused by the auto-mode classifier in a different container; it was not refused here, so the denial is container- or posture-dependent, not a property of the gh path.
- The Bash tool's worktree-isolation guard refuses several ordinary shell shapes in this worktree: a `for` loop calling gh with a loop variable, a `cat > file <<'EOF'` heredoc, and `tail -c 60 file | od -c`. Scratch files had to be written with the Write tool and REST pagination issued one page per command. Any skill or runbook that tells a worktree agent to script these will stall; the workable spellings are plain single commands plus `python3 -c` for byte work.
- FOLLOW-UPS.md is append-only by convention and nothing trims or indexes it: it is now 121 KB on this branch (eight `## Run (ticket-fleet)` sections plus two triage ledgers), single-line bullets up to 1.4 KB, and no tool reads it. Wave-5 bullet W5-20 is the first case of an entry that is now factually contradicted by shipped code and could only be annotated, not corrected. Worth deciding whether triaged run sections should be pruned once every bullet has a disposition.

## Run (ticket-fleet)

- ASSUMPTION (issue 360 ambiguity): the ticket offers two alternative fixes and I implemented the first - the Report phase cuts `agent/fleet-discoveries-wf_<runId>` from origin/<defaultBranch> in a scratch worktree, commits FOLLOW-UPS.md there alone, and opens a discoveries-only PR. The second half (the chore naming each discovery sha) is not code in this repo because nothing in claude-dotfiles files the triage chore - the fleet script only returns findings. I covered it by returning `discoveryReport` ({branch, sha, prUrl, bullets}) from the run and documenting in orchestrator/RUNBOOK.md that a chore filed for the bullets names that sha and branch. If the chore-filing step lives in a surface outside this repo (a Routine prompt, the master orchestrator's own session text), that surface still needs editing to actually print the sha.
- The discoveries branch is named `agent/fleet-discoveries-wf_<runId>`, which deliberately does NOT match `agent/issue-*` - the glob orchestrator/RUNBOOK.md's merge pass uses to find fleet PRs, and the shape tools/backfill-worktree-configs.js and the branch tests assume. I added an explicit RUNBOOK rule so a human/agent merge pass picks it up, but any automation that filters on the literal `agent/issue-` prefix will skip the discoveries PR and strand the bullets again. Worth a follow-up to widen those filters to `agent/` or to register the discoveries prefix wherever fleet branches are enumerated.

Issue 360 explicitly asks to 'strike or annotate' the block, so I treated the ticket as the exception and inserted a blockquote above the six issue-120 bullets rather than editing or deleting any of them. If the append-only rule is meant to be absolute, the annotation would instead have to be a new dated bullet at the end of the file pointing back at the stale block.
- The four commits cited by the issue-120 block are confirmed absent from this repository's object store (`git cat-file -t` fails on 2fc568c, 8675994, bdf0ed1 and 0327175), and issue 120 is still open - so the skill rewrites, stamp rotation and marketplace rebuild those bullets report as done were never landed.
