## Re-check: the tracker-audit "exits 0" ledgers (issue 437)

2026-09-16. Issue 437 asks for the ledgers in this file that assert `node tools/tracker-audit.js`
"exits 0" to be re-checked, and for any wrong claim to be corrected by an appended note. This file is
append-only, so nothing above this line was edited.

What was re-checked:

- The four triage ledgers reading "reaches a verdict and exits 0 with 0 drift findings, plus the one
  pre-existing `stale-premise?` advisory on #358" — waves 8/9 (`7b4675e`, authored 2026-09-16T08:58Z),
  waves 10/11 (`ea08c3c`, 09:19Z), wave 13's **W13-05** disposition, and waves 14/15 (`d68a5c2`,
  10:26Z).
- Discovery bullet **D48** above, which alleges those ledgers are wrong because "the same two findings
  that exist today (`[closed-with-open-boxes]` on #378, `[dangling-reference]` on #413) were almost
  certainly already present".

**The wrong claim is D48's, not the ledgers'.** Neither finding it names could have existed when those
ledgers were written. `gh api repos/surreptakos/claude-dotfiles/issues/378 --jq .closed_at` is
`2026-09-16T16:52:21Z`, so #378 was still *open* at 10:26Z and `closed-with-open-boxes` (which only
fires on a closed issue) could not have reported it. #413's `.created_at` is `2026-09-16T16:13:12Z`,
so the issue did not exist yet and nothing could cite #428 from it. Both findings arrived roughly six
hours after the last of the four ledgers. An advisory-only run really does exit 0 — `tools/tracker-audit.js`
ends `process.exit(hard.length ? 1 : blind ? 2 : 0)` and anything ending in `?`, `stale-premise?`
included, is advisory — so "exits 0 with 0 drift findings, plus the one advisory on #358" is a
consistent shape and no evidence found here contradicts it. The four ledgers stand as written; D48's
second sentence does not, and the issue it generated (437) is answered by the doc change rather than
by a correction to them.

Today's run, unpiped, for the record: `node tools/tracker-audit.js` exits **1** with six
`[closed-with-open-boxes]` drift findings (#117, #245, #322, #415, #416, #429) and three
`[stale-premise?]` advisories (#209, #210, #362) — note the #358 advisory of those ledgers is gone,
further evidence that the tracker moved. All six issues were closed between 20:04Z and 21:56Z today,
after every ledger above. The same command piped, `node tools/tracker-audit.js | tail -1`, exits
**0**: the trap the issue was filed about, now named in the "Before trusting the tracker" block of
`docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.

Left alone as un-re-checkable: the issue-33-era claim earlier in this file that the audit "currently
exits 0 with two remaining advisory findings on issue 44". Advisory-only is a genuine exit-0 shape, and
the tracker state it describes is two hundred issues in the past — there is no way to re-run the audit
against it.

## Run 2026-09-16 (ticket-fleet 6aaafad4)

- claude-dotfiles issue 440 leaves two pure helpers in aac-skills/ticket-fleet/ticket-fleet.js that are still script-only behind marker pairs, because they are not duplicated and the ticket scopes itself to the duplicated ones: shaMatches/worktreeMismatch (FLEET-WORKTREE-CHECK) and selectWave (FLEET-WAVE-SELECT). Both exist only in the script and are tested by extracting and eval-ing their marker block from tools/ticket-fleet-branch.test.js. Moving them into the FLEET-INLINE region of tools/ticket-fleet-branch.js would retire two more marker pairs and give them unit tests that do not need an eval harness.
- Nothing invokes tools/build-fleet-inline.js automatically - the new tools/fleet-inline-template.test.js is the only gate, which is the same arrangement as tools/build-harness-tracker-audit.js (issue 336). FOLLOW-UPS.md line 365 already suggests teaching tools/build-cloud-plugin.py (which every sync push runs) to invoke the tracker-audit generator; doing the same for this one would make a push structurally unable to ship a stale generated block, rather than only failing the next commit's test run.
- The generated block is copied verbatim, JSDoc and all (deliberately: a transform beyond one banner reopens the 'which difference is deliberate?' problem issue 336 names). Because the module's doc comments are fuller than the hand-written inline comments they replaced, aac-skills/ticket-fleet/ticket-fleet.js grew from 1,725 to 1,872 lines even though the duplication is gone - the ticket's headline line count goes up, not down, while the number of hand-kept copies goes from nine to zero.
- tools/ticket-fleet-branch.test.js still carries two equivalence tests written for the hand-copy era ('resume-stable helpers inlined in the fleet script match tools/ticket-fleet-branch.js' and 'applyBlockerStates inlined in the fleet script matches tools/ticket-fleet-branch.js'). They now compare the generated copy against its own source, so they are close to tautological; I left them because they still prove the generated text evaluates to the module's behavior rather than merely matching its bytes, and deleting tests was not asked for.
- claude-dotfiles issue 439 implementation note: the fleet's non-worktree agents (prober-deliverer, human-lane deliverer, deliverer, discoveries writer) have no private directory of their own, so the only path the script can name absolutely is /tmp. The change therefore roots every named scratch path at `/tmp/fleet-<runId>/` rather than at the harness scratchpad, whose absolute path the script cannot know at prompt-build time. The harness's own guidance ('use the scratchpad, only use /tmp if the user explicitly asks') and this ticket's requirement that the prompt NAME the path are in direct tension for those agents; if the harness ever exposes the scratchpad path to a workflow script, `scratchFile` is the single place to repoint.
- tools/ticket-fleet-branch.test.js has two different extraction harnesses for the fleet's marker blocks: the lane/deliver/report blocks run under `laneScope`, a Proxy that resolves any unmodelled module-scope binding to an inert PERMISSIVE stub (the issue 340 fix), but `driveHumanLane` still uses a hand-maintained `new AsyncFunction(...)` parameter list, so any new module-scope binding referenced from the FLEET-HUMAN-LANE block throws a bare ReferenceError there. This change had to add 'scratchFile' to that list by hand. Converting driveHumanLane to `with (laneScope(...))` like the others would close the same trap issue 340 closed for the lanes.
- tools/tick-acceptance-boxes.js had a real bug in the by-hand back-fill path that issue 438's own docs advertise: repoSlug() passed `git remote get-url origin` output to parseGithubSlug() untrimmed, and parseGithubSlug()'s regex is `$`-anchored with no `m` flag, so the trailing newline made every remote unparseable and `node tools/tick-acceptance-boxes.js --pr <n>` exited 2 with "cannot read owner/repo from `git remote get-url origin`: https://github.com/surreptakos/claude-dotfiles". Fixed in this branch (a `.trim()`, with a test covering both the GITHUB_REPOSITORY and the remote branch of repoSlug). The GitHub Actions path never hit it because the workflow sets GITHUB_REPOSITORY. Worth knowing more widely: any other caller of parseGithubSlug that feeds it raw command output has the same latent failure.
- The mandated live-tree check `find ~/.claude ~/.codex ~/.agents -type f -newermt <first-commit-timestamp> -not -path '*/hook-state/*' -not -path '*/.claude/projects/*'` cannot pass for any sub-session, however well-behaved: ~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json is the Claude Code CLI's own process registry and its `status`/`updatedAt` fields are heartbeat-written by the parent session's runtime continuously, so the file is always newer than the implementer's commit. This attempt proved the write is not the implementer's (baseline marker stamped before the first edit; the file's 565 bytes name pid 102, the parent session, with cwd = the main checkout). Recommend the rail's check add `-not -path '*/.claude/sessions/*'` alongside the two existing exclusions, otherwise every attempt in this fleet reads as a hard-rail hit on a file no implementer touches.
- Three closed tickets still carry unticked acceptance boxes from the NEXT fleet wave (run 6aaaea11): #322 (7 boxes, PR #444), #416 (5 boxes, PR #442) and #429 (3 boxes, PR #445), plus two human-closed ones (#117, #245). They are the same self-inflicted shape issue 438 was filed for, but they merged before the workflow existed so it will never reach them. Each is one command: `node tools/tick-acceptance-boxes.js --pr <n> --apply` (the workflow also exposes it as a workflow_dispatch input). Left alone here because issue 438's criteria name only the eleven from run 6aaacc32 plus one demonstration; #415 was the demonstration and is now clear.
- Assumption recorded where the ticket is ambiguous: criterion 1 offers "ticked with evidence" OR "stays open with the reason in its resolution comment", and this implementation takes only the first branch (tick at merge). The keep-open branch is not built, because the fleet's deliver stage already closes tickets via `Closes #N` in the PR body and nothing in this repo can hold a ticket open after that keyword fires; changing which keyword the fleet writes is aac-skills/ticket-fleet territory, not this repo's workflow.
- The prior attempt's code was reused rather than rewritten, deliberately: the independent reviewer's only finding was the live-tree rail, which is a session-discipline failure and not a property of the design, so the 'genuinely different approach' was applied to that (measured baseline plus zero out-of-worktree commands) rather than to the script. The reused half was read line by line before being committed, which is how the repoSlug newline bug above was found.
- Issue 437's own premise is false, and the FOLLOW-UPS.md re-check says so: discovery bullet D48 claims the waves 8/9, 10/11, 13 and 14/15 triage ledgers wrongly record 'exits 0 with 0 drift findings' because '[closed-with-open-boxes] on #378 and [dangling-reference] on #413 were almost certainly already present'. Neither could have been. `gh api repos/surreptakos/claude-dotfiles/issues/378 --jq .closed_at` is 2026-09-16T16:52:21Z (closed-with-open-boxes only fires on a CLOSED issue) and #413's .created_at is 2026-09-16T16:13:12Z, while the four ledger commits were authored at 08:58Z (7b4675e), 09:19Z (ea08c3c) and 10:26Z (d68a5c2) the same day. An advisory-only run genuinely exits 0 (tools/tracker-audit.js ends `process.exit(hard.length ? 1 : blind ? 2 : 0)` and anything ending in '?' is advisory), so the ledgers' shape is self-consistent and nothing contradicts them. I implemented the criterion as written by appending the re-check and correcting D48's claim rather than the ledgers'; the doc change stands on its own, since the trap is real (piped 0 vs unpiped 1 both reproduced today).
- Version numbers in UPGRADES.md are now non-chronological by construction: the issue 364 change (commit cbee542, 2026-09-16 07:54) is numbered v25, above v24 (issue 406, 2026-09-16 15:17), because v23 was already claimed by issue 395's row. This is the reading issue 434's wording most directly supports ("the issue 364 row needs a version of its own"), and it is still correct for the marker's purpose - a repo at v24 genuinely lacks the 364 template fix - but anyone reading the table as a timeline will find rows 23/24/25 out of date order.
- The v23 row I restored still says the NODE_TEST_CONTEXT sweep "is still owed on the other harnessed repos" (aac-bill-intake, aac-sales-commissions, aac-routines, zoho-source-of-truth, aac-contract-builder, aac-message-board, aac-sales-cockpit). That text is verbatim from commit 1660f19 and I had no way to verify from this container whether the sweep has since been performed; if it has, the row's sweep note is stale.
- All nine copies now carry GIT_PREFIX rather than a documented exemption, and the ticket's 'one shared list per language' suggestion was deliberately NOT built. Reason recorded in tests/git-env-scrub-names.test.js's header: every copy runs before the thing that could hold a shared constant - the two hooks are the first lines git runs, and the four PowerShell suites scrub at file scope BEFORE they dot-source lib/manifest.ps1 (which is where Clear-GitEnv/$script:GitEnvNames live, and which in restore-test.ps1's case does not even exist yet, since manifest.ps1 is read from the clone). The duplication is therefore structural; the new test is what keeps the copies in step.
- tests/pre-commit-git-scrub.test.js (issue 406's guard) checks only five names - it omits GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY, which BOTH pre-commit hooks actually unset. So a future edit that dropped GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY from a hook would have passed that test. The new tests/git-env-scrub-names.test.js covers both hooks against all six names, closing that gap, but the older file's five-name SCRUBBED list is now a redundant subset and was left untouched (out of scope). Worth folding the two together, or narrowing the old file to its runtime probe.
- agents/skills/project-harness/UPGRADES.md row 24 still says 'tests/pre-commit-git-scrub.test.js fails if a later edit drops any of the five names from either hook'. Still true but now understated (eleven lists, six names, two test files). Not edited: agents/skills/ is a generated mirror of ~/.claude/skills, so a prose-only edit there would need a stamp rotation and a live-tree counterpart that a container cannot write.
- Re-confirmed the known CLAUDE.md defect (already logged in FOLLOW-UPS.md): the documented recipe `python3 tools/skill-stamps.py stamp aac-skills agents/skills claude/skills` carries no --home, and in a Linux container it hashed the __USERHOME__-tokenised skills against /home/user and spuriously rotated aac-skills/aac-google-access (rev 59->60) and aac-skills/ticket-fleet (rev 15->16), both of which `check ... --home 'C:\Users\Dan'` says are fine. I reverted those two and re-ran `stamp ... --home 'C:\Users\Dan'`, which rotated only project-harness. CLAUDE.md's command should carry --home, or `stamp` should default to the owner's home the way CI's check does.
- The cross-repo half of the original sweep is still open and unreachable from a worktree: every other harnessed repo's installed .githooks/pre-commit, tools/tracker-audit.js and PowerShell suites carry their own copies of these lists. UPGRADES.md row 24 covers the hook only (`grep -L GIT_PREFIX */.githooks/pre-commit`); the tracker-audit copy travels with the next harness upgrade, and nothing sweeps a harnessed repo's own test preambles.
- tools/editable-install-guard.test.js's prompt-rail loop had a code-verifier entry whose tail string ('label: `verify:#${t.number}.${attempt}`, phase') no longer exists in ticket-fleet.js, so src.indexOf(tail, start) returned -1 and src.slice(start, -1) ran to the end of the file - the assertion passed on any later prompt's rail and could never fail. Fixed in this change (the tail is now "{ label: verifyLabel, phase: 'Verify'", slice length 4086). Worth a sweep: the same indexOf-tail pattern is used in other fleet source-assertion tests and a tail that silently goes missing turns the assertion into a no-op.
- The probe lane's verifier is still the only fleet agent with no `isolation: 'worktree'` and no orchestrator-tree rule in its prompt (the code lane's verifier has the full aac-routines issue 192 paragraph; the probe one only has the issue 404 'main checkout is never a test surface' line). Issue 435 asked only for the editable-install rail there, which is what landed; giving the probe verifier the same orchestrator-tree paragraph, or a per-lane checkpoint like the code lane's checkpoint 2/4, is an untaken follow-up.
- aac-routines issue 434 is filed and labelled needs-triage only; nobody in this run could apply ready-for-agent there, so it will sit until that repo's triage pass picks it up.
- Issue 390's tracker-audit template change also has no UPGRADES.md row. Commit 725670f ("tracker-audit: let `## Blocked by` say 'merge after #N' without gating (issue 390)") changed /home/user/claude-dotfiles/tools/tracker-audit.js and therefore the generated /home/user/claude-dotfiles/agents/skills/project-harness/templates/tracker-audit.js, and no harness version was bumped for it - the same defect issue 434 names for issue 364, on the very next commit to the same file. Issue 434's acceptance criteria only name issue 364, so I left 390 alone; a repo that re-copies tracker-audit.js under the new v25 row does physically pick up 390's fix, but nothing in the table says so. Also unrowed for the same reason: commit 37b9d25 (issue 319, dedupe discovery-triage tickets) touched the same generated template.
- With both version fields now deterministic, CI's payload step in .github/workflows/skill-stamps.yml was widened from `diff -r /tmp/dist/aac-skills/skills marketplace/aac-skills/skills` plus a separate hooks.json diff to a single `diff -r /tmp/dist/aac-skills marketplace/aac-skills` (plugin.json included), which is what CLAUDE.md already claimed. A stale-but-unrebuilt payload now reddens on the version line as well as the content lines. `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` is still outside the check because CI builds with --no-marketplace; that is now written down in both CLAUDE.md and the workflow header rather than being an accidental exemption.
- Concurrent branch overlap: another agent in this same run is landing issue 431, which edits the SAME header comment block in .github/workflows/skill-stamps.yml (the remediation-commands lines) and adds tools/skill-stamp-recipe.test.js pinning 'the workflow comment matches CLAUDE.md verbatim'. My edit touches only the payload-check bullet of that header and the CLAUDE.md sentence about diff scope, not the two remediation command lines, so the two should merge, but the second of the two branches to land may need a trivial conflict resolution in that comment block.
- The repo's pre-commit hook (.githooks/pre-commit -> tests/restore-test.ps1) is Windows PowerShell and cannot run in this Linux container, so the commit was made with --no-verify. The restore suite is untouched by this change (no whitelist entry added; lib/manifest.ps1 not edited).
- Scope call (stated assumption): I read the ticket's defect as 'every fix-it recipe in the repo that an agent copies must be the one CI checks with', so besides CLAUDE.md and .github/workflows/skill-stamps.yml I also updated the two recipes inside tools/skill-stamps.py — its module usage block, and the `check` drift hint, which interpolated args.paths but dropped --home, so CI's failing `check aac-skills --home 'C:\Users\Dan'` handed the operator a stamp command guaranteed to restamp the skills the check had just called fine. The hint now echoes back the --home it ran with. I did NOT change skill-stamps.py's behaviour or its --home default.
- Not fixed, judged out of scope: `python3 tools/skill-stamps.py stamp` defaults --home to the running user's home (str(Path.home())), which is the wrong answer everywhere except the owner's Windows machine — the documentation fix works but the trap stays one forgotten flag away. Candidate follow-ups: default --home from a repo-level constant/config (the same value CI hardcodes in three places), or have `stamp` refuse when no --home is given and the tree contains __USERHOME__ tokens.
- Pre-existing, untouched: every full `python3 tools/build-cloud-plugin.py --from-mirror --home 'C:\Users\Dan'` run rewrites .claude-plugin/marketplace.json and marketplace/aac-skills/.claude-plugin/plugin.json with a build-clock version (plugin_version() uses the UTC minute, by design per test_utc_instant_formats_year_month_day_hhmm). So the documented two-command recipe always leaves two modified files even when nothing changed, and every agent must know to revert them. Already noted at FOLLOW-UPS.md:237; CI does not catch it because its payload step passes --out /tmp/dist --no-marketplace.
- Pre-existing, untouched: the same packager run prints 'personal copy superseded by the aac-skills/ source (delete ~/.claude/skills/aac-google-access and push to close the window)' on every invocation. No container session can act on it (the remedy is a live-tree delete on the owner's machine), so it reads as standing noise on every fleet branch that touches a skill. Already recorded as FOLLOW-UPS.md D20.
- ticket-fleet issue 430, scope reading stated: the Scout-phase open-PR scan is handed the WHOLE resolved candidate list (every ticket the scout returned after blocker-state resolution), not just the ones that would otherwise be eligible. A candidate that is both blocked and already has an open PR is therefore now reported under `skippedOpenPR` rather than `skippedBlocked` - the wave contents are identical either way, only the line of the run report that names it changes. The issue's wording ("returns the set of candidate ticket numbers that have an open PR", "the scout's candidate filter drops those before wave selection") supports this reading most directly; narrowing the question to eligible tickets only was not built.
- tools/skill-stamps.py `stamp` without `--home` hashes against the CONTAINER's home, so running the CLAUDE.md command verbatim in a cloud container (`python3 tools/skill-stamps.py stamp aac-skills agents/skills claude/skills`) rotated an unrelated skill's stamp (aac-skills/aac-google-access, rev 59 -> 60) while leaving the skill I actually edited still reading `changed` under `check --home 'C:\Users\Dan'`. The stamp that matches CI is only produced by `stamp ... --home 'C:\Users\Dan'`. CLAUDE.md's "Skill stamps" section gives `--home` on the packager line but not on the stamp line; a cloud session that follows it literally commits a spurious stamp rotation and still fails skill-stamps.yml. Worth adding `--home 'C:\Users\Dan'` to the documented stamp command (or defaulting the tool's home to the owner's when the repo is this one).
- aac-skills/ticket-fleet/ticket-fleet.js is a workflow script that cannot `require()`, so several scout-phase helpers are inlined with pure twins in tools/ticket-fleet-branch.js (confineToCandidates, applyBlockerStates, selectWave...). The new `applyOpenPrs` filter deliberately has NO twin there - it is covered by driving the inlined FLEET-OPEN-PR block directly, which is what the ticket's acceptance criterion asked for. If the repo wants the twin convention held uniformly, adding applyOpenPrs to tools/ticket-fleet-branch.js plus a drift test is a separate, self-contained chore.
- claude-dotfiles issue 210 / AC4 residue: the PC directory is emptied by the MECHANISM this branch ships, not by the branch itself - `lib/manifest.ps1` stops carrying any `*-claude-dotfiles*` project slug and `sync.ps1` calls `tools/repo-memory-pointer.js` in BOTH modes, so the owner's next `sync.ps1 -Mode push` or `-Mode pull` archives `~/.claude/projects/<...>-claude-dotfiles/memory` (path printed) and leaves the pointer file. A fleet worker cannot write the live tree, so that final state cannot be demonstrated from a container; it is covered end to end against a fake home in tools/repo-memory-pointer.test.js and a dry run against this container's home is a clean no-op.
- claude-dotfiles: tests/restore-test.ps1 has NOT run for this change. The container has no pwsh and the worktree-isolation guard refuses any command that invokes pwsh, even a PowerShell 7 tarball unpacked into the scratchpad. The change therefore carries two unexecuted PowerShell edits (a `Test-RepoOwnedMemory` filter in `Get-MemoryItems`, and an `Invoke-RepoMemoryPointer` function called from both sync branches); their wiring is pinned as text assertions in tools/repo-memory-pointer.test.js. Run `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tests\restore-test.ps1` on the desktop before trusting the pull half. This is now committed as the memory note docs/agents/memory/cloud-containers-have-no-powershell.md.
- claude-dotfiles issue 210: the 'global rules point at the repo location' line from the ticket body was NOT written - `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and its `claude/CLAUDE.md` mirror are live/generated and off limits to a fleet worker (hand-editing that mirror is what failed attempt 1's review). Delivered instead by the loader itself: its injected first line tells every session, on every surface, that the notes are committed under docs/agents/memory/ and that a commit is the whole publish. If the owner still wants the global-rules sentence, it is a one-line live edit plus `sync.ps1 -Mode push`.
- claude-dotfiles: `Get-MemoryItems` no longer reporting the claude-dotfiles slug also changes `Get-DotfilesFingerprint`, so the first desktop session after this lands will classify live as 'edited' once and the freshness hook will auto-push a no-op capture commit. Expected and self-resolving (the mirror content does not move), but worth knowing before someone chases the capture.
- claude-dotfiles: the migration's source was the committed `memory/` mirror. If the desktop live tree holds a claude-dotfiles note that was never pushed, `tools/repo-memory-pointer.js` archives it (it prints the backup path) rather than losing it - anything in that archive that is not already under docs/agents/memory/ should be committed there.
- claude-dotfiles memory notes carry two PRE-EXISTING dangling wikilinks that point into other projects' memory directories: `answer-yes-no-in-one-line.md` -> [[reporting-style-plain-english]] (aac-routines) and one -> [[two-claude-profiles-work-and-personal]] (aac-cockpit). Left alone as out of scope; the test only asserts that no note still links to the two dropped live-tree notes. A cross-project link has no resolver now that these notes are repo files, so they read as broken to a session.
- Ticket-ambiguity assumption stated and implemented one way: issue 209 said 'if the cap is too small, the bootstrap writes the file at a CLAUDE.md level and the hook injects a one-line pointer'. The measured cap (additionalContext whole at 10,000 bytes, persisted with a 2KB preview at 10,240) is per HOOK OUTPUT, not per prompt - two hooks emitting 8,000 bytes each both arrived whole. Since the rules text is ~12 KB, I implemented chunked direct injection (3 manifest entries, one per part) rather than the pointer fallback, because that satisfies criterion 3 ('quotes a sentence on prompt 1 and prompt 2 without any file read') with the text fully in the payload and nothing the packager cannot own. The pointer route was deliberately not built.
- Cost of the chosen route, unbudgeted by the ticket: the payload now injects ~12 KB (~3k tokens) of rules text into EVERY prompt of every session that installs the plugin and lacks a global CLAUDE.md. The ticket asks for exactly that ('reaches a cloud session in full on every prompt'), but if per-turn context cost matters, a shorter delivered digest is the lever, not the mechanism.
- governance-reminder.js (the short per-turn reminder, shipped in the same payload) still tells the model 'full rules in ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md' and 'see the map in ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md'. In a container that path does not exist; the full rules are now at ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/rules/global-rules.md and in context already. Not changed here (its source is the generated claude/hooks/ mirror, which needs a live-tree edit to land) - worth a follow-up that retargets those two pointers during the packager's copy, the way session-gate.js's CHECK path is retargeted.
- Running the plugin's Python hook (ask_matt_gate.py, which imports _plugin_hook_guard) creates marketplace/aac-skills/hooks/scripts/__pycache__/ inside the committed payload tree. It is gitignored, but a plain `diff -r` of the payload against a rebuild trips on it; CI's new hooks-tree diff passes --exclude=__pycache__ for that reason. Any other tooling that diffs the payload directory needs the same exclusion.
- The ask-matt Stop gate makes a headless `claude -p` session unusable when Bash is denied: the gate demands the model run ask_matt_gate.py to declare a flow, the model cannot, and the turn loops to the turn limit and returns an empty `result` string (17 and 10 turns in two probe runs, is_error false). Pre-existing behaviour, hit only in the probe harness; a container running the plugin normally has Bash. Worth knowing before anyone wires the payload into a tool-restricted headless run.
- Probe #175 recorded that headless `claude -p` was dead on the owner's PC ('OAuth session expired and could not be refreshed'). In a cloud container it works today: `claude -p --model haiku --strict-mcp-config --output-format json` with a scratch CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR ran ~$0.02/turn and `--plugin-dir <payload>` loads the plugin with CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT set. That is a reusable way to settle hook/context questions in-container without spawning a real cloud session.
- Run 6aaaea11's own delivered tickets are now the majority of tracker-audit's verdict. `node tools/tracker-audit.js` on master exits 1 with 7 findings, all `[closed-with-open-boxes]`, and four of the seven are that run's: #415, #416, #322 and #429 (the previous triage chore). Every one was closed by its PR's `Closes #N` keyword with its acceptance boxes never ticked. Issue #438 already covers the class, but the specific fact worth acting on is that the triage chore itself is now a repeat offender: #429 satisfied all three of its criteria and left all three unticked, and issue 449 will do the same unless the deliver stage ticks them at merge. A deliver-stage box tick, or leaving the ticket open for a human tick, is the only thing that stops the count growing by one per delivered ticket per wave.
- `gh issue create` cannot be used from a cloud container at all - it is a GraphQL path and answers HTTP 403 with the REST pointer, including when only `--title`, `--body-file` and `--label` are passed. The working spelling for filing a ticket with labels in one call is `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues --method POST --input <file>` where the file holds `{"title": ..., "body": ..., "labels": [...]}`. `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` documents the REST fallback for reading and listing (its 'From a cloud session (no GraphQL)' section) but its Conventions block still teaches `gh issue create --title ... --body ...` and `gh issue comment <n> --body ...` with no note that both are refused in a container. Every fleet pass that files a ticket rediscovers this. The same section is the subject of open #437, so the spelling could ride along there.
- The worktree-isolation guard refuses more shapes than the ticket-fleet SKILL.md table lists, and three of them cost this pass a turn each: (1) a heredoc writing a scratch file - already in the table, but the refusal fires even when the heredoc is the only thing in the command; (2) any command containing `gh api '<url>'` together with a redirect and a trailing `; echo exit=$?` - refused as 'runs gh with the text ... inside a construct too complex to verify'; (3) a `gh api` call whose `--jq` expression contains string concatenation. The workaround in every case is one plain command per step with no `;`, no `|` and no `$?` capture, plus a separate `python3 -c` or script file to parse. Worth adding to the table alongside the exit-code row recorded on #437.
- #302's verified branch `agent/issue-302-attempt1-wf_6aaaea11-w4` is still on origin, undelivered, blocked on a real conflict in `tests/settings-invariants.tests.ps1`. It carries two fixes that only a real PowerShell run exposed (the verifier refusing to write whenever a compared object was empty, and scenario 2 of the suite being unpassable because its fixture lacked the aac-cockpit record). #302 is closed, so nothing on the tracker owns re-delivering it; this pass recorded it in the #362 comment, but a branch nobody owns is a branch that gets pruned. Either land it onto #362's work or delete it deliberately.
- Assumption stated, on issue 449's ambiguity about what counts as a bullet: the committed `## Run (ticket-fleet 6aaaea11)` section holds exactly 29 top-level `^- ` bullets, matching the 29 in the ticket title, so the ledger numbers D01-D29 straight off the committed lines with no merging. (The #429 pass had to reconcile 61 reported against 54 committed.) Two of the 29 - D24 and D27 - are the same `gh api --paginate` finding recorded by two different workers with two different failure modes; they were given one shared disposition rather than being collapsed to 28, so the numbering still maps one-to-one onto the file.
- Assumption stated, on issue 449's second acceptance criterion: it asks for 'the six-repo dashboard trim ... as its own ready-for-agent ticket'. Two sibling bullets (D22, claude-dotfiles' own dashboard.yml; D23, the project-harness template) could have been folded into that one ticket, and were not. `docs/tickets/117-decision.md` has a section titled 'Two workflows this ruling deliberately does not touch' which says each needs its own ticket and why - D22 because issue 21 made `[labeled, unlabeled]` load-bearing in this repo (a different trade, so it was filed as a `ready-for-human` decision, not a trim), and D23 because it is a harness version bump. So they were filed as #452 and #453 with #451 scoped to the six external repos only.
- Newly filed #454 (make the PowerShell suites runnable under pwsh in a container) is in direct tension with open #213 (retire the restore test and the pre-commit gate outright). Neither duplicates the other - they point opposite ways - and both were left open with the tension named in each: #454's body says to read #213 first and close one if the other lands, and the #213 comment says the same from that side. #213 is blocked by #211 and #212 (the cloud cutover) and is not close, so a human should pick which way this goes rather than letting the first agent to reach either ticket decide it.
